Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in. I, um... I hope I'm explaining this right, and I really don't want you to think badly of me or think I'm crazy. But I'm here because... well, at work—I'm a nursing assistant—I've been having this issue. 

I'm sure it's probably just stress or being overtired, but I keep hearing these voices. They come from the call-light speaker at the desk, and sometimes from the hallway vents. They keep telling me which patient records have been changed. And I know how strange that sounds, I really do, but it's just as clear as somebody standing right next to me. Well, um... I guess my mood does change a little bit sometimes. I really hope this doesn't make me sound difficult to work with, because I try so hard to be a good team player. Oh, um... empty? No, I don't think I've ever felt empty. I mean, sometimes I get a little worn out, just physically tired from being on my feet at the hospital all day. But not sad or... or empty for the whole day.

I hope that's the right answer... I mean, I hope that's what you were asking. I just want to be helpful. Is that what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Are the voices something you hear all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Well, um... they definitely come and go. It's not all the time at all. I mean, most of my shift I'm just working normally, taking care of my patients, doing everything I'm supposed to do. \n\nIt's usually just... maybe when it's really late, or if I'm particularly tired? I'm pretty sure it's just a fatigue thing. It's not like they're bothering me constantly or keeping me from doing my job well. I'm still a very good worker, I really am. \n\nSo, yes, they just come and go. I hope that makes sense? Did I explain that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly at different times?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess my mood does change a little bit sometimes. I really hope this doesn't make me sound difficult to work with, because I try so hard to be a good team player. But... some days I just wake up and I feel like I'm finally, truly awake, you know? Like I suddenly have all this clarity and energy, and I feel like I'm meant to go in and just fix everything at work. I just want everything to run perfectly for the patients. \n\nBut the problem is... and I feel terrible admitting this to you... if my husband, or maybe one of the nurses on shift, tells me to slow down or take a breath, I can get a little... well, I snap at them. I don't mean to be mean, I promise. It's just in that moment, I feel like they're blocking something really important that I'm supposed to be doing. \n\nI always feel awful about it later, because I really hate being rude to people. Is that... does that answer your question? I just want to make sure I'm explaining things the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control when you're in one of those high-energy, clarity moments?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'd never want you to think I'm running wild or anything like that. \n\nIt's more just... I have so much energy during those times. Like, a really productive amount of energy! I'll work a full shift at the hospital, and instead of being tired, I'll come home and just... keep going. I'll clean the whole house, or I'll sit down and rewrite all my notes so they're perfectly neat, or I'll reorganize all our supplies. \n\nSometimes I'll just be pacing around the kitchen, trying to figure out what to fix next, and my husband will be practically begging me to just sit down and rest. I'm doing okay, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel extremely down or sad for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. Not extremely down, anyway. Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I ever lose interest in things. I mean, I love my hobbies. I love baking for the nurses' station and taking care of my garden.

I'm pretty sure it's just a fatigue thing. It's not like they're bothering me constantly or keeping me from doing my job well. I'm still a very good worker, I really am. \n\nSo, yes, they just come and go. I hope that makes sense? Did I explain that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly at different times?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess my mood does change a little bit sometimes. Like I suddenly have all this clarity and energy, and I feel like I'm meant to go in and just fix everything at work. I just want everything to run perfectly for the patients. \n\nBut the problem is... and I feel terrible admitting this to you... if my husband, or maybe one of the nurses on shift, tells me to slow down or take a breath, I can get a little... well, I snap at them. Is that... does that answer your question? I just want to make sure I'm explaining things the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control when you're in one of those high-energy, clarity moments?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Well, um... happy? Yes, I suppose I do have days where I feel very happy. I mean, it's a good thing to be happy, right? 

Sometimes I wake up and I just feel... like I'm finally fully awake, you know? Like I have all this wonderful energy and I'm meant to go into the hospital and just... fix everything. But, um... sometimes, if my husband or one of the charge nurses tells me to slow down or take a break... I guess I can get a little, well, snappy. I don't mean to! I just feel like they're getting in the way of something really important I'm supposed to be doing. I always apologize afterward, of course. I hate being sharp with people. 

Is it... is it bad that I get a little irritable when I'm trying to help? I just get so focused on doing a good job. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a difficult person to work with...

Oh, um... regret? No, I really don't think so. I mean, I try very hard to always be a good person, you know?

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and answering your question properly.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear ever tell you to do something, rather than just giving you information?\n\nPatient: No, no, they don't do that. I mean, they never tell me to do anything bad or anything like that. I would never listen if they did, I promise you. I take my job very seriously, and I always follow the rules. \n\nThey just... they just tell me things. Like I said, about the records. I just listen, I don't act on it or anything. I hope that's the right answer... I mean, I hope that's what you were asking. I just want to be helpful. Is that what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Are the voices something you hear all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Well, um... they definitely come and go. It's not all the time at all. I mean, most of my shift I'm just working normally, taking care of my patients, doing everything I'm supposed to do. \n\nIt's usually just... maybe when it's really late, or if I'm particularly tired? I'm pretty sure it's just a fatigue thing. It's not like they're bothering me constantly or keeping me from doing my job well. I'm still a very good worker, I really am. \n\nSo, yes, they just come and go. I hope that makes sense? Did I explain that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly at different times?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess my mood does change a little bit sometimes. I really hope this doesn't make me sound difficult to work with, because I try so hard to be a good team player. But... some days I just wake up and I feel like I'm finally, truly awake, you know? Like I suddenly have all this clarity and energy, and I feel like I'm meant to go in and just fix everything at work. I just want everything to run perfectly for the patients. \n\nBut the problem is... and I feel terrible admitting this to you... if my husband, or maybe one of the nurses on shift, tells me to slow down or take a breath, I can get a little... well, I snap at them. I don't mean to be mean, I promise. It's just in that moment, I feel like they're blocking something really important that I'm supposed to be doing. \n\nI always feel awful about it later, because I really hate being rude to people. Is that... does that answer your question? I just want to make sure I'm explaining things the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control when you're in one of those high-energy, clarity moments?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm *not* in control. I'm always very careful to stay in control, especially because of my job. I'd never want you to think I'm running wild or anything like that. \n\nIt's more just... I have so much energy during those times. Like, a really productive amount of energy! I'll work a full shift at the hospital, and instead of being tired, I'll come home and just... keep going. I'll clean the whole house, or I'll sit down and rewrite all my notes so they're perfectly neat, or I'll reorganize all our supplies. \n\nSometimes I'll just be pacing around the kitchen, trying to figure out what to fix next, and my husband will be practically begging me to just sit down and rest. But I'm completely in control of it, I'm just... being very thorough. I like to be helpful, you know? It's a good thing to be productive, right? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm out of control. I'm doing okay, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel extremely down or sad for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. Not extremely down, anyway. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm, you know, a gloomy person or not capable of doing my job with a good attitude. I don't mean to! I just feel like they're getting in the way of something really important I'm supposed to be doing. I always apologize afterward, of course. I hate being sharp with people. \n\nIs it... is it bad that I get a little irritable when I'm trying to help? I just get so focused on doing a good job. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a difficult person to work with...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these really energetic and productive times are separate from your normal self, or are they just a part of who you are all the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I like to think it's just me, you know? Just me having a really good day where I'm finally living up to my potential. \n\nI mean, everyone has days where they're more productive than others, don't they? I just... I really want to be the best nursing assistant I can be, and the best wife. So when I have those days with all that energy, I just feel like I'm finally doing what I'm supposed to do. Like I'm finally getting it right. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm some sort of Jekyll and Hyde person, because I'm really not. I'm always just... me. Just trying to be helpful and do a good job. It's just that sometimes I have a little more pep in my step. That's a normal thing, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever do things during those highly energetic times that you later regret?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... regret? No, I really don't think so. I mean, I try very hard to always be a good person, you know? \n\nLike I was saying, I mostly just use that extra energy to work harder at the hospital or get things done around the house. I wouldn't do anything... well, anything bad or reckless, if that's what you're asking. I take my responsibilities as a wife and a nursing assistant very, very seriously. \n\nI suppose sometimes I might stay up a little too late scrubbing the kitchen or baking, and then maybe I'm a bit physically tired the next day, but... that's not really something to regret, is it? Because it's all for a good cause. I just want to be helpful and do things right. \n\nI hope that makes sense. I'd hate for you to think I was out doing irresponsible things or making a mess of my life. I really do try my best to be reliable. Is... is that what you meant?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like these energetic and productive times happen in cycles, or are they more random?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 50 / nursing_assistant / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Cycles? Um, well... I'm not entirely sure what you mean by that. I don't think I've really noticed a pattern or anything like that. 

I think it's mostly just random, you know? Like, sometimes I'll just wake up and feel really good and ready to take on the day. Maybe the sun is shining, or I know it's going to be a busy shift at the hospital and they really need me. It just... happens when it happens, I guess. 

I wouldn't say it's like a clockwork thing or anything that I can predict. I just try to make the most of those good days when they come around. That's the right way to handle it, don't you think? Just... being grateful for the energy when it's there? I hope I'm explaining this okay... I just haven't really kept track of it like that.

High? Um... well, I guess I'd say I just feel... really awake. Like my eyes are finally open and I'm just meant to go in and fix everything at work that needs fixing. It's a wonderful feeling, honestly, because I'm able to be so helpful to everyone.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Always in a good mood? Oh, well... I try my best to be! I really do think a smile goes a long way, especially working with the patients. But, um, I wouldn't say I'm *always* in a perfect mood. I mean, nobody's perfect, right? 

Sometimes if it's just a regular day and I'm not feeling that... that big burst of energy, I might just feel, well, normal. Or maybe a little quiet. Just an average day, I guess. If I'm a little worn out from my shifts, I might not be practically humming with happiness, but I try very hard not to let it show. I would never want to bring anyone else down just because I'm having a quiet day. 

I think it's just so important to keep things pleasant for everyone else, you know? Even if you're just feeling okay, you should still put your best foot forward. That's the right way to be, don't you think? Just trying to be a positive person for the people around you?

Without thinking them through? Oh, um... no, I really don't think I do that. I try to be a very responsible person, you know?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Less sleep? Oh, well... I suppose I might sleep a little bit less, but not in a bad way! It's not like I'm tossing and turning or struggling with insomnia. I hate the idea of wasting time in bed when there's so much good work to be done. 

When I have all that wonderful energy, I just... I wake up feeling so refreshed and ready to go, even if it's only been a few hours. I feel completely rested, I really do. It's actually quite nice because it gives me extra time to get things ready for my husband or maybe pick up an extra shift at the hospital. They're always so short-staffed, and I like to be the one they can rely on. 

I don't think it's a problem to need a little less sleep if you're still functioning perfectly well and helping people, right? It just means I'm making the most of my day. That's a good thing, isn't it?

Spend more money? Oh, um... no, not really. I mean, I'm very careful with our finances. My husband works so hard, and I would never want to be wasteful, you know?

I'll work a full shift at the hospital, and instead of being tired, I'll come home and just... keep going. I'll clean the whole house, or I'll sit down and rewrite all my notes so they're perfectly neat, or I'll reorganize all our supplies. \n\nSometimes I'll just be pacing around the kitchen, trying to figure out what to fix next, and my husband will be practically begging me to just sit down and rest. But I'm completely in control of it, I'm just... being very thorough. I like to be helpful, you know? It's a good thing to be productive, right? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm out of control. I'm doing okay, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel extremely down or sad for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. Not extremely down, anyway. I mean, everybody has days where they feel a little tired or... or maybe just quiet, right? But I always try to keep a positive attitude. \n\nIt's so important for my patients to see a smiling face, so I really make sure I don't let myself get sad like that. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm, you know, a gloomy person or not capable of doing my job with a good attitude. I try my absolute best to be cheerful for everyone around me. \n\nI'm a very positive person, mostly. Is that... is that okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt sad or empty for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... empty? No, I don't think I've ever felt empty. I mean, sometimes I get a little worn out, just physically tired from being on my feet at the hospital all day. But not sad or... or empty for the whole day. \n\nI have so much to be grateful for, you know? My husband, my job... I always try to count my blessings. I really believe that if you just keep busy and keep a smile on your face, you don't really have time to sit around feeling sad. I just focus on being there for my patients and my family. \n\nI hope that's okay to say? I just... I wouldn't want you to think I'm hiding anything from you, I promise I'm not. I just really try to be a happy person. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you lost interest in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I ever lose interest in things. I mean, I love my hobbies. I love baking for the nurses' station and taking care of my garden. \n\nSometimes, if I've been working a lot of overtime, I might not have the energy to get out in the yard on my day off. But that's just because I'm being responsible and resting my body so I can be at my best for my patients the next day. It's not that I don't *want* to do those things, I just... I prioritize my responsibilities, you know? \n\nI always try to stay engaged and keep up with my interests. I think it's so important to have a well-rounded life. I wouldn't want to be someone who just gives up on the things they love. That sounds awful, doesn't it? I really do try to stay active and involved in everything I enjoy. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel extremely happy or elated for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... happy? Yes, I suppose I do have days where I feel very happy. I mean, it's a good thing to be happy, right? \n\nSometimes I wake up and I just feel... like I'm finally fully awake, you know? Like I have all this wonderful energy and I'm meant to go into the hospital and just... fix everything. Organize the supply closets, cheer up all the patients, just make everything perfect. It feels really good to be that helpful. \n\nBut, um... sometimes, if my husband or one of the charge nurses tells me to slow down or take a break... I guess I can get a little, well, snappy. I don't mean to! I just feel like they're getting in the way of something really important I'm supposed to be doing. I always apologize afterward, of course. I hate being sharp with people. \n\nIs it... is it bad that I get a little irritable when I'm trying to help? I just get so focused on doing a good job. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a difficult person to work with...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these really energetic and productive times are separate from your normal self, or are they just a part of who you are all the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I like to think it's just me, you know? Just me having a really good day where I'm finally living up to my potential. \n\nI mean, everyone has days where they're more productive than others, don't they? I just... I really want to be the best nursing assistant I can be, and the best wife. So when I have those days with all that energy, I just feel like I'm finally doing what I'm supposed to do. Like I'm finally getting it right. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm some sort of Jekyll and Hyde person, because I'm really not. I'm always just... me. Just trying to be helpful and do a good job. It's just that sometimes I have a little more pep in my step. That's a normal thing, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever do things during those highly energetic times that you later regret?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... regret? No, I really don't think so. I mean, I try very hard to always be a good person, you know? \n\nLike I was saying, I mostly just use that extra energy to work harder at the hospital or get things done around the house. I wouldn't do anything... well, anything bad or reckless, if that's what you're asking. I take my responsibilities as a wife and a nursing assistant very, very seriously. \n\nI suppose sometimes I might stay up a little too late scrubbing the kitchen or baking, and then maybe I'm a bit physically tired the next day, but... that's not really something to regret, is it? Because it's all for a good cause. I just want to be helpful and do things right. \n\nI hope that makes sense. I'd hate for you to think I was out doing irresponsible things or making a mess of my life. I really do try my best to be reliable. I'm not entirely sure what you mean by that. I don't think I've really noticed a pattern or anything like that. \n\nI think it's mostly just random, you know? Like, sometimes I'll just wake up and feel really good and ready to take on the day. Maybe the sun is shining, or I know it's going to be a busy shift at the hospital and they really need me. It just... happens when it happens, I guess. \n\nI wouldn't say it's like a clockwork thing or anything that I can predict. I just try to make the most of those good days when they come around. That's the right way to handle it, don't you think? Just... being grateful for the energy when it's there? I hope I'm explaining this okay... I just haven't really kept track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mood is particularly high or elevated when you're having those very energetic and productive times?\n\nPatient: High? Um... well, I guess I'd say I just feel... really awake. Like my eyes are finally open and I'm just meant to go in and fix everything at work that needs fixing. It's a wonderful feeling, honestly, because I'm able to be so helpful to everyone. \n\nThough, I suppose... well, sometimes if I'm in the middle of getting all those important things done, and my husband or maybe one of the charge nurses tells me I need to slow down... I might get a tiny bit frustrated? I might snap at them just a little bit. Not because I'm a mean person! I would never want you to think that. It's just... it feels like they're blocking something really important that I'm supposed to be doing, you know? \n\nI always feel terrible if I'm short with anyone, and I really don't mean to be rude. It's just that I'm trying so hard to do a good job for the patients and my family. That's understandable, isn't it? I just get a little too focused on helping.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt sad or depressed for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Sad? Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. I try my best to be! I really do think a smile goes a long way, especially working with the patients. But, um, I wouldn't say I'm *always* in a perfect mood. Extremely happy or irritable? Well... I wouldn't say "extremely," no. That makes it sound so... out of control. But I suppose there are days—sometimes a few days in a row—where I just feel... awake. Like I'm finally meant to step up and fix everything at work, you know? Like I can just see exactly what needs to be done on the floor and I have the energy to do it. 

It's a wonderful feeling, really, being that capable. But... well, I suppose I do get a tiny bit frustrated sometimes. If my husband tells me I need to sit down and rest, or if one of the other nurses tells me to slow down when I'm just trying to help... I might snap at them a little bit. It's just because I feel like they're blocking something important that I'm supposed to be doing, and I don't want to lose my momentum. 

I always apologize afterward, of course. I don't like being sharp with people. It's just that I get so focused on doing a good job. That's understandable, isn't it? To get a little testy when people interrupt your work?
